Topic: a story about an unusual holiday trip

Text:
Last summer, my friend Tom and I decided to try something different for our holiday. Normally, we go to the beach, but this time, we followed Tom’s suggestion and visited a small village inside the mountains. The owner of a local guesthouse told us about a hidden method to explore the area: hiking up a steep hill to a secret viewpoint.

At first, I was nervous. The path was narrow, and I kept asking myself, "What if I get lost?" But Tom laughed and said, "Don’t worry! Just follow me!" Halfway up, my stomach started hurting—probably from the altitude—but we kept going. When we reached the top, we saw an informal picnic spot with amazing views. The owner of the guesthouse had left us a basket of food as a surprise!

We spent the afternoon there, eating and relaxing. It was the best holiday trip we’d ever had—unusual, but unforgettable.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Tom and the narrator choose a different holiday destination this time?
Correct: Because Tom suggested visiting a mountain village.
Distractors: They wanted to try something new and exciting. / They heard about a cheaper place to stay. / They were invited by a friend who lived there.
Explanation:
The text states: "Tom’s suggestion and visited a small village inside the mountains." While "trying something new" is true, it is not the reason given in the text. The other options ("cheaper place," "invited by a friend") are not mentioned.
---
Q2: What problem did the narrator face while hiking up the hill?
Correct: Their stomach hurt because of the altitude.
Distractors: They got lost on the narrow path. / They were too tired to continue. / They argued with Tom about the route.
Explanation:
The text says: "Halfway up, my stomach started hurting—probably from the altitude." The other options are incorrect because the narrator followed Tom, didn’t mention tiredness, and didn’t argue.
---
Q3: What did the narrator find at the top of the hill?
Correct: An informal picnic spot with food left by the guesthouse owner.
Distractors: A group of tourists waiting for them. / A sign warning about dangerous animals. / A small café selling drinks.
Explanation:
The text describes: "an informal picnic spot... The owner of the guesthouse had left us a basket of food." The other options are not supported by the text.



Topic: a story about an unusual family celebration

Text:
Every year, the Chen family celebrates their anniversary in a very unusual way. Instead of a big party, they gather in the garden at sunset and share stories. This year, they decided to try something new: each person had to write a short essay about their favorite family memory. The variety of topics was surprising—some wrote about funny moments, others about deep emotions. Even Mr. Chen, who is usually quiet, shared a story about a time his father taught him to swim.

The family argued about which story was the best, but in the end, neither could decide. Instead, they lit candles and read the essays aloud under the stars. The light from the candles made the garden feel magical. The celebration wasn’t completely silent—there was laughter, tears, and even a few jokes about Uncle Li’s terrible cooking. By the end, everyone agreed: this was the most meaningful anniversary they’d ever had.

Questions:
Q1: What did the Chen family do differently this year for their anniversary?
Correct: They wrote essays about their favorite family memories.
Distractors: They traveled to a new country. / They invited all their neighbors. / They cooked a special dinner together.
Explanation:
The text says they wrote essays about favorite memories, so the correct answer is clear. The other options are not mentioned—there’s no travel, neighbors, or special dinner described.
---
Q2: Why did the family argue during the celebration?
Correct: They couldn’t agree on which story was the best.
Distractors: They disagreed about the direction of the candles. / They argued about who wrote the longest essay. / They couldn’t decide which profile to take for the event.
Explanation:
The text states they argued about which story was best, so the correct answer fits perfectly. The other options are unrelated—direction of candles, essay length, or profile are not mentioned.
---
Q3: What made the celebration feel special?
Correct: The light from the candles and reading stories aloud.
Distractors: A variety of foods was served. / They watched a movie together. / They played music completely silently.
Explanation:
The text describes the candles’ light and reading stories as key to the magic. The other options are incorrect—no food, movie, or silent music is mentioned. The celebration was lively, not silent.



Topic: a description of a morning routine that makes me happy

Text:
My morning routine makes my heart happy! First, I wake up early and go outside to breathe fresh air. The sun rises over the lake, and I feel like saying, "Wow!" The water is calm, and birds sing beautifully. Next, I take a short walk around the lake to protect nature—no litter, just peace. Then, I do some light exercise: stretching, jumping, and running a little. After that, I have a healthy breakfast with fruit and tea. It’s all right to relax for a few minutes before starting my day. Finally, I check my goals for the day and take one step at a time. If I reach them, I feel like a celebrity—happy and proud!

Questions:
Q1: What does the writer do first after waking up?
Correct: Go outside to breathe fresh air.
Distractors: Drink coffee / Read a book / Listen to music.
Explanation: The text says, "First, I wake up early and go outside to breathe fresh air." Coffee, reading, or music are not mentioned.
---
Q2: Why does the writer walk around the lake?
Correct: To protect nature.
Distractors: To take photos / To meet friends / To exercise more.
Explanation: The text states, "I take a short walk around the lake to protect nature." Taking photos, meeting friends, or extra exercise are not mentioned.
---
Q3: How does the writer feel after completing their morning goals?
Correct: Like a celebrity.
Distractors: Tired / Excited / Sad.
Explanation: The text says, "If I reach them, I feel like a celebrity—happy and proud!" Tired, excited, or sad are not described as the final feeling.



Topic: a funny story that happened to my friends and me

Text:
Last summer, my twin brother and I went camping with our friends. We planned a fun trip, but everything went wrong! First, we forgot the double amount of food, so we had to share one small plate of sweet biscuits for breakfast. Then, our celebrity chef friend (who loves cooking) argued with us about how to make the best campfire soup. The character of our trip changed completely—we spent more time chatting about silly things than cooking!

The worst part? We tried to increase the fun by telling ghost stories, but one friend fell asleep, and another started laughing so hard he dropped his plate! By the end, we all agreed: next time, we’ll hire a real chef instead of bringing a "celebrity" who can’t cook!

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator and his friends have only one small plate of biscuits for breakfast?
Correct: They forgot to bring enough food.
Distractors: They shared their food with other campers. / They ate all the biscuits the night before. / They wanted to save money for snacks later.
Explanation:
The text says, "We forgot the double amount of food," meaning they didn’t bring enough. The other options are incorrect because:
- Sharing with campers isn’t mentioned.
- Eating all biscuits the night before contradicts the idea of saving them for breakfast.
- Saving money isn’t the reason—they simply forgot the food.
---
Q2: What happened during the ghost story part of the trip?
Correct: One friend fell asleep, and another laughed so hard he dropped his plate.
Distractors: They all got scared and ran away. / They decided to tell real ghost stories instead. / They argued about who had the best story.
Explanation:
The text states: "one friend fell asleep, and another started laughing so hard he dropped his plate." The other options are wrong because:
- No one ran away—it was a funny, not scary, moment.
- They didn’t switch to real stories; the ghost stories were already silly.
- They didn’t argue—they just laughed and messed up.
---
Q3: Why did the narrator say they should hire a real chef next time?
Correct: Their "celebrity" chef friend couldn’t cook well.
Distractors: They wanted to impress their friends with professional food. / The campfire soup was too spicy. / They didn’t like camping anymore.
Explanation:
The text explains: "our celebrity chef friend (who loves cooking) argued with us about how to make the best campfire soup," implying the food was bad. The other options are incorrect because:
- Impressing friends isn’t mentioned—they just wanted good food.
- Spiciness isn’t mentioned.
- They didn’t dislike camping; they just had a bad cooking experience.



Topic: an adventure story about getting lost in the forest

Text:
Last autumn, Jake went hiking in the forest with his friends. It was his birthday, and they gave him a new compass as a gift. The weather was perfect, but after a few hours, Jake realized they were lost. The trees looked the same everywhere, and his phone had no network. His friends were nervous, but Jake stayed calm. "We need to find a direct path," he said. They checked their equipment—maps, water, and snacks—but nothing helped. Jake noticed a broken branch pointing almost north. "Follow this!" he shouted. They walked for an hour and finally saw a road. Jake’s sock was torn, but he was happy. They were safe, and his friends thanked him for staying brave.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Jake and his friends get lost in the forest?
Correct: They walked too far from the path.
Distractors: Their compass was broken. / They ignored Jake’s advice. / The forest was too small to get lost in.
Explanation:
The text says they were hiking and "realized they were lost," but it doesn’t mention a broken compass or ignoring advice. The forest isn’t described as small, so getting lost is plausible. The most logical reason is that they walked too far from the path, which is a common way to get lost in a forest.
---
Q2: What did Jake use to help them find the way out?
Correct: A broken branch pointing almost north.
Distractors: His torn sock. / The gift from his friends. / The forest network of trails.
Explanation:
The text states Jake "noticed a broken branch pointing almost north" and used it to guide them. His torn sock was just a detail about his condition, not a tool. The "gift" (compass) didn’t help because the phone had no network, and the forest isn’t described as having a helpful trail network.
---
Q3: How did Jake’s friends feel when they were lost?
Correct: They were nervous.
Distractors: They were excited. / They were angry at Jake. / They were too tired to care.
Explanation:
The text clearly says, "His friends were nervous." There’s no mention of excitement, anger, or tiredness—just nervousness, which is a natural reaction to being lost in the forest.



Topic: an adventure story about finding a secret door in the house

Text:
Last summer, I moved into an old house with my family. My son, who loves cartoons and creative stories, noticed something strange: behind a weak bookshelf in the living room, there was a small, hidden door. At first, I didn’t believe him, but when I pushed the shelf, the door opened! Inside, we found old papers with strange symbols and a diary from a businessman who lived there 50 years ago. The diary mentioned a secret room full of "evidence" about a treasure.

My wife wasn’t sure about going inside, but my son and I decided to explore. We followed the clues in the diary and found a locked box under the floorboards. Inside was a key and a note: "Only those with a strong relationship with adventure will find the treasure." My son laughed and said, "I guess we’re lucky!" We still haven’t found the treasure yet, but the mystery keeps us excited. Maybe one day, we’ll recognize the hidden clues and solve the puzzle!

Questions:
Q1: What did the narrator’s son find behind the bookshelf?
Correct: A small, hidden door
Distractors: A locked box / A cartoon character / A diary from a businessman
Explanation:
The text says, "My son... noticed something strange: behind a weak bookshelf in the living room, there was a small, hidden door." The correct answer directly matches this detail. The locked box was found later under the floorboards, not behind the bookshelf. The cartoon character is mentioned as something the son loves but not as something found in the house. The diary was discovered inside the secret room, not immediately behind the bookshelf.
---
Q2: Why did the narrator’s wife hesitate to explore the secret room?
Correct: She wasn’t sure about going inside.
Distractors: She didn’t believe the son’s story. / She was afraid of the weak bookshelf. / She wanted to call the businessman’s family first.
Explanation:
The text states, "My wife wasn’t sure about going inside," which matches the correct answer. The other options are incorrect because the wife did not doubt the son’s story (the narrator initially did), there is no mention of her fearing the bookshelf, and the text does not say she wanted to contact the businessman’s family.
---
Q3: What did the note inside the locked box say?
Correct: "Only those with a strong relationship with adventure will find the treasure."
Distractors: "The treasure is hidden under the floorboards." / "This key opens the door to a creative mind." / "I was lucky to find this evidence."
Explanation:
The text includes the exact quote from the note: "Only those with a strong relationship with adventure will find the treasure." The other options are incorrect because the treasure was not mentioned as being under the floorboards (the key was), the note did not mention a "creative mind," and the word "lucky" was used by the son, not in the note. The note refers to adventure, not luck.



Topic: an adventure story about finding a treasure

Text:
One sunny afternoon, Tom found an old map in his grandfather’s pocket. It showed a hidden treasure near the beach, marked by a round stone with a column of numbers carved into it. The map was covered in strange symbols, but Tom’s friend, Lily, suggested they look for the treasure together. They followed the clues to a cave, where waves crashed against the rocks. Inside, they found a chest with gold coins and a note from a long-lost king. The note said the treasure belonged to anyone who could solve the final riddle: "Only the brave possess true wealth." Tom and Lily decided to share the treasure and use part of it to help their village. They also found a sort of advertising poster in the cave, warning others not to take what wasn’t theirs.

Questions:
Q1: What did Tom find in his grandfather’s pocket?
Correct: an old map
Distractors: a key / a letter / a coin
Explanation: The text clearly states Tom found "an old map" in his grandfather’s pocket. The other options (key, letter, coin) are not mentioned and do not fit the context of the treasure hunt.
---
Q2: What did the note in the treasure chest say?
Correct: "Only the brave possess true wealth."
Distractors: "The treasure is for the king." / "Take half and leave the rest." / "This belongs to the village."
Explanation: The text includes the exact quote from the note: "Only the brave possess true wealth." The other options are plausible but not supported by the text. The note does not mention the king, splitting the treasure, or the village’s ownership.
---
Q3: What did Tom and Lily decide to do with the treasure?
Correct: share it and help their village
Distractors: keep it all for themselves / sell it / bury it again
Explanation: The text says they "decided to share the treasure and use part of it to help their village." The other options (keeping it, selling it, burying it) contradict the text, which emphasizes sharing and helping others.



Topic: a story about a day when everything went wrong but ended well

Text:
Last autumn, during the lovely but rainy season, Jake, an active young soldier, woke up with a terrible illness. He had a high fever and a sore throat, but his task was to lead a morning patrol. His captain said, "You need to go—it’s important!" Jake felt weak, but he didn’t want to let his team down.

At the bar near the base, he stopped for coffee. A stranger there was a doctor. "You look terrible!" she said. "Rest today!" But Jake insisted on finishing his task. By midday, he was running at full speed, but his legs felt heavy. Just as he reached the checkpoint, he collapsed.

The doctor rushed to help. "You pushed too hard!" she scolded. Jake smiled weakly. "I’m okay now." The team carried him back, and the doctor gave him medicine. That evening, Jake slept peacefully—his illness gone. Actually, it turned out he had just needed rest. His team cheered when he woke up the next morning, healthy and ready to train again.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Jake go on patrol even though he was sick?
Correct: His captain told him it was important.
Distractors: He wanted to prove he was strong. / He didn’t want to miss the lovely weather. / He thought the bar was closed.
Explanation:
The text says, "His captain said, 'You need to go—it’s important!'", so Jake went because his task was urgent. The other options are incorrect: the text doesn’t mention proving strength, the weather being a reason, or the bar being closed.
---
Q2: What did the doctor at the bar tell Jake to do?
Correct: Rest for the day.
Distractors: Drink more water. / Take medicine immediately. / Walk slower next time.
Explanation:
The doctor said, "Rest today!", so the correct answer is clear. The other options are not mentioned in the text.
---
Q3: How did Jake’s day end?
Correct: He slept peacefully and felt better the next day.
Distractors: He had to stay in the hospital. / His team punished him for going out. / He still had a fever at night.
Explanation:
The text states, "That evening, Jake slept peacefully—his illness gone." The other options contradict this: there’s no mention of a hospital, punishment, or lingering fever.



Topic: a story about a time I helped a neighbor

Text:
Last month, I received an invitation from my neighbor, Mr. Thompson, a busy businessman. He asked if I could help him move a thick bookshelf. At first, I wasn’t sure how to act, but I decided to help. When I arrived, I saw two other neighbors already there. Together, we lifted the shelf easily. Mr. Thompson was very grateful and even offered me coffee. Later, I realized that helping him was a good factor in making our neighborhood friendlier. I also figured out that small acts of kindness can make a big difference in people’s lives.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator decide to help Mr. Thompson?
Correct: He wanted to be kind and friendly.
Distractors: He needed money for the help. / He was forced by his boss. / He was afraid of Mr. Thompson.
Explanation: The text says the narrator "decided to help" and later mentions that small acts of kindness make a difference, showing his kindness. There is no mention of money, a boss, or fear.
---
Q2: What did the narrator learn from helping Mr. Thompson?
Correct: Small acts of kindness can make a big difference.
Distractors: Moving heavy furniture is easy. / Neighbors should always ask for help. / Businessmen are always unfriendly.
Explanation: The text states, "I also figured out that small acts of kindness can make a big difference in people’s lives." Moving furniture is not the main lesson, and the text does not say neighbors should always ask for help or that businessmen are unfriendly.
---
Q3: What did Mr. Thompson do after the narrator helped him?
Correct: He offered the narrator coffee.
Distractors: He gave the narrator a rate for the work. / He invited the narrator to a party. / He suggested they move another shelf.
Explanation: The text says, "Mr. Thompson was very grateful and even offered me coffee." There is no mention of a rate, a party, or moving another shelf.



Topic: a story about my favorite toy from childhood

Text:
When I was a child, my favorite toy was a small train set. It had an incredible design with colorful passengers and a long track that wound around my room. My father helped me build it, and I spent hours watching the train move. One day, I accidentally dropped a knife (it was part of a toy tool set) near the track, and the train stopped immediately. I was so scared! My father laughed and said, "Don’t worry, we’ll fix it!" He showed me how to carefully remove the knife and clean the track again. After that, I learned to be more careful with my toys.

Years later, I wanted to publish a book about my childhood memories. I included a drawing of my train set and wrote about the process of building it. My friends were surprised when they read it—"Ah!" they said, "You never told us about this!" Now, whenever I see a train, I remember that special time.

Questions:
Q1: What happened when the boy dropped the knife near the train track?
Correct: The train stopped immediately.
Distractors: The train fell off the track. / The passengers disappeared. / The track broke into pieces.
Explanation: The text says, "the train stopped immediately" after the knife was dropped. The other options are not mentioned—there is no mention of the train falling, passengers disappearing, or the track breaking.
---
Q2: Why did the boy’s father help him fix the train?
Correct: Because the boy was scared and needed support.
Distractors: Because the train was too expensive to repair. / Because the boy’s mother asked him to. / Because the train was broken beyond repair.
Explanation: The text states, "I was so scared!" and "My father laughed and said, 'Don’t worry, we’ll fix it!'", showing that the father helped because the boy needed reassurance. The other options are not supported by the text—there is no mention of cost, the mother’s involvement, or the train being beyond repair.
---
Q3: What did the boy later decide to do with his childhood memory?
Correct: He published a book about it.
Distractors: He gave the train set to a museum. / He sold the train set online. / He forgot about it completely.
Explanation: The text says, "I wanted to publish a book about my childhood memories." The other options are not mentioned—there is no reference to donating the train, selling it, or forgetting about it.



Topic: a story about my first day at school

Text:
My first day at school was scary! My parents were divorced, so I lived with my mum. She told me to wash my hands before breakfast and hide my fears. I packed my bag with a new diary and a pencil. The school was round the corner, but I felt nervous.

At school, the coach showed us the playground. We played games, and I tried a fun experiment with colours. The teacher asked for our choice of lunch—pizza or soup. I chose pizza! After class, I almost fell while running, but my friends helped me up. By the end of the day, I wasn’t so scared anymore.

Questions:
Q1: What did the narrator’s mother tell him to do before breakfast?
Correct: Wash his hands.
Distractors: Hide his diary / Serve breakfast / Experiment with colours.
Explanation:
The text says, “She told me to wash my hands before breakfast.” The other options are incorrect because hide refers to fears, not the diary; serve breakfast is not mentioned; and experiment with colours happens at school, not before breakfast.
---
Q2: What did the narrator bring to school in his bag?
Correct: A new diary and a pencil.
Distractors: A coach / A choice of lunch / A round playground.
Explanation:
The text states, “I packed my bag with a new diary and a pencil.” The other options are incorrect because a coach is a person at school, not an item; choice of lunch is an option given by the teacher, not packed; and the round playground is described but not brought in a bag.
---
Q3: Why did the narrator feel nervous on his first day?
Correct: Because his parents were divorced.
Distractors: Because he fell during class / Because he didn’t like the experiment / Because he couldn’t serve lunch.
Explanation:
The text explains, “My parents were divorced, so I lived with my mum.” The other options are incorrect because falling happened later, not the cause of nerves; disliking the experiment is not mentioned; and serving lunch is irrelevant to his nerves.



Topic: a story about a best friend I met when I was little

Text:
When I was five, my family moved to a new house near a big department store. One day, I lost my mother on the crowded stair in the basement. Suddenly, a boy with bright red hair appeared. "Don’t worry," he said, "I’ll help you find her." His name was Leo, and he became my best friend.

Leo was clever—he could print perfect drawings of cars and planes. He also loved racing toy cars, but he never won because he always let others go first. "It’s more fun to share," he’d say. One summer, we found a chance to earn cash by selling his drawings in the store’s small shop. According to Leo, we should give half to charity. He seemed kinder than anyone I knew.

Years later, Leo became an engineer, but we still meet for coffee. He always says, "Friendship is like a good race—it’s about the journey, not the prize."

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator and Leo earn money in the department store?
Correct: They sold Leo’s drawings.
Distractors: They cleaned the store’s windows. / They helped customers find products. / They delivered packages to other floors.
Explanation: The text says, "we found a chance to earn cash by selling his drawings in the store’s small shop." Cleaning windows, helping customers, or delivering packages are not mentioned. The correct answer directly matches the text’s detail about selling drawings.
---
Q2: What did Leo do with the money they earned?
Correct: He suggested giving half to charity.
Distractors: He kept all of it for himself. / They spent it on toys. / They saved it for a trip.
Explanation: The text states, "According to Leo, we should give half to charity." The other options contradict this—Leo was generous, not selfish, and the text doesn’t mention spending money on toys or saving for a trip.
---
Q3: How did Leo behave during toy car races?
Correct: He let others win on purpose.
Distractors: He always cheated to win. / He never participated. / He only raced against his friends.
Explanation: The text says, "he never won because he always let others go first." This proves he didn’t cheat or refuse to race; he was selfless. The other options are unsupported by the text.



Topic: a story about a birthday party I will never forget

Text:
Last year, my best friend turned 18, and we planned a birthday party like no other. The general idea was to surprise her with a movie premiere—her favorite director was coming! We decorated the room like a cinema, even involving her pet dog in the fun. The night before, she was asleep when we arrived, so we hid quietly.

At midnight, we woke her up with balloons and music. She screamed with joy—her heart was full of happiness! The director arrived early and recorded a special message for her. We even played a game where everyone had to guess her personality based on clues. It was so much fun that we forgot time was flying by.

The best part? We made a record of the night with photos and videos. It was necessary to celebrate her in such a special way—she’ll never forget it, and neither will I!

Questions:
Q1: What was the main surprise for the birthday girl?
Correct: A movie premiere with her favorite director.
Distractors: A trip to the zoo with her pet dog. / A sleepover with all her friends. / A game where everyone had to guess her favorite food.
Explanation: The text says the party was planned like a "movie premiere" and that her "favorite director was coming." The other options are not mentioned: there was no trip to the zoo, sleepover, or food-guessing game.
---
Q2: Why did the friends hide before waking her up?
Correct: She was asleep when they arrived.
Distractors: They wanted to scare her as a joke. / They were waiting for the director to arrive first. / They were afraid she would cry.
Explanation: The text states, "The night before, she was asleep when we arrived, so we hid quietly." The other options are incorrect because there’s no mention of scaring her, waiting for the director, or fearing tears.
---
Q3: What did the group do to remember the party?
Correct: They made a record with photos and videos.
Distractors: They wrote a letter to her. / They baked a cake with her favorite song. / They sang her a song every year after.
Explanation: The text says, "We made a record of the night with photos and videos." The other options are not supported by the text: no letter, cake, or annual singing is mentioned.



Topic: a story about helping a lost pet find its home

Text:
Last week, a comedy actor named Tom—everyone calls him "the guy"—found a lost cat under his apartment carpet. The cat was thin and scared, but it had a collar with a phone number. Tom knew he had to help. He called the number and learned the cat belonged to an old lady who lived alone. She was incredible—she had been searching for her pet for weeks!

Tom suggested they meet at the park. He even brought treats to make the cat feel safe. The lady was so happy to see her pet again! "This is a huge benefit for both of us," she said. "I can’t replace the time I lost, but at least my cat is back."

Tom also trained the cat to recognize its name again. Now, the lady visits him every Sunday to give him a quantity of cookies as thanks. "Helping animals is one of the best things including in my life," Tom says.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Tom call the number on the cat’s collar?
Correct: To find out who the cat belonged to.
Distractors: To ask if the cat needed food. / To tell the owner about a lost dog. / To ask for money in exchange for the cat.
Explanation:
The text says Tom "called the number and learned the cat belonged to an old lady." This means he wanted to find the owner. The other options are incorrect because the text does not mention food, another dog, or asking for money.
---
Q2: What did Tom bring to help the cat feel safe?
Correct: Treats.
Distractors: A blanket. / A toy. / A leash.
Explanation:
The text states, "He even brought treats to make the cat feel safe." A blanket, toy, or leash are not mentioned.
---
Q3: What did the lady say was a "huge benefit"?
Correct: Finding her cat again.
Distractors: Tom’s training the cat. / The cookies she gave Tom. / The comedy actor’s help.
Explanation:
The lady said, "This is a huge benefit for both of us," referring to getting her cat back. While Tom’s training and the cookies were part of the story, the key benefit was reuniting with her pet. The "comedy actor’s help" is too general and not directly quoted.



Topic: a story about the first time I used a video call to talk to a family member far away

Text:
Last year, my grandmother moved to another city, and I missed seeing her. One day, my mother said, “We need a helpful instrument to stay united!” She taught me how to use video calls. At first, I was nervous—what if there was a danger with the technology? But my mother smiled and said, “Don’t worry! It’s general knowledge now.” The first time I called Grandma, I wore my best clothes because I wanted to suit the occasion. She looked happy, and we talked for an hour. Video calls gave us peace of mind, knowing we could see each other even from far away. Now, my whole family uses them, and society has changed because of this helpful instrument. It may not be perfect, but it keeps us close.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator’s mother suggest using video calls?
Correct: To stay connected with family members who live far away.
Distractors: To teach the narrator how to use new technology. / To replace phone calls completely. / To help the narrator practice speaking English.
Explanation:
The text says, “We need a helpful instrument to stay united!” This shows the mother wanted to keep the family connected. The other options are incorrect because the focus is on staying close, not teaching technology, replacing phone calls, or practicing English.
---
Q2: What was the narrator’s first concern about video calls?
Correct: There might be a danger with the technology.
Distractors: The video quality might be poor. / The call might be too expensive. / The family might not like it.
Explanation:
The narrator thought, “what if there was a danger with the technology?” The other options are not mentioned in the text. The text does not discuss video quality, cost, or family preferences.
---
Q3: How did video calls change the narrator’s family?

Topic: a story about a traditional festival in my country

Text:
Every year in Vietnam, the Mid-Autumn Festival is celebrated with great joy. This typical holiday happens since September to October, when families gather to enjoy the moon and share delicious food. One of the most exciting parts is the variety of colorful lanterns that light up the streets. Children love playing with them, and some even carry small units of fireworks for fun.

A special tradition is making mooncakes, sweet pastries filled with different flavors like lotus, peanut, or bean. Some families also avoid eating certain foods, like insect-based snacks, because they believe it brings bad luck. The festival also has games, such as guessing lantern riddles, where people answer questions written on paper.

Research shows that this festival has been celebrated for over 1,000 years! The most famous story is about Chang’e, a female astronaut who flew to the moon. People say she still lives there, and that’s why the moon looks so bright during this time. The festival is also a time to remember family and friends, making it a special day for everyone.

Distractors: They want to eat lighter meals. / The foods are too expensive. / The foods are not traditional.
Explanation:
The text states: "Some families also avoid eating certain foods, like insect-based snacks, because they believe it brings bad luck." This means the reason is superstition, not weight or cost. The other options are not mentioned and contradict the idea of tradition.

Distractors: Eating only sweet foods. / Watching fireworks shows. / Making mooncakes.

While fireworks and mooncakes are part of the festival, the typical child activity is playing with lanterns. Eating only sweet foods is not mentioned, and making mooncakes is usually an adult task.

Topic: a funny story about a kitchen accident

Text:
Last weekend, my friend Mark and I decided to invite ourselves to a cooking competition. We wanted to make chips, but we didn’t have enough time to cook them properly. Mark sat at the table while I stood at the stove, but neither of us knew how to use the oven well. I turned the heat too high, and suddenly, the kitchen filled with smoke. The fire alarm started ringing, and we panicked. We tried to put out the fire, but we only made it worse. The chips were destroyed, and the smoke detector’s battery was dead—we couldn’t even hear it anymore!

Then, Mark slipped on a banana peel (yes, we had one on the floor) and fell into the sink. We laughed so hard that we forgot about the fire. The neighbors heard us and knocked on the door. They discovered us sitting on the floor, covered in flour, with a half-burnt pan in our hands. They clearly thought we were in a fight, but we just said, “We’re fine! We’re just… cooking!”

Questions:

Correct: They didn’t know how to use the oven properly.
Distractors: They forgot to buy potatoes. / They didn’t have enough time to clean the kitchen. / They used the wrong type of oil.
Explanation:
The text says, “we didn’t have enough time to cook them properly” and “Mark sat at the table while I stood at the stove, but neither of us knew how to use the oven well.” This means they failed because of their lack of oven skills, not because they forgot potatoes, didn’t clean, or used the wrong oil (which isn’t mentioned).
---
Q2: What happened when the fire alarm rang?
Correct: They panicked and tried (but failed) to put out the fire.
Distractors: They called the fire department immediately. / They ignored it because the battery was dead. / They blamed each other for the accident.
Explanation:
The text states, “We tried to put out the fire, but we only made it worse.” They didn’t call the fire department (no mention of it), didn’t ignore it (they did react), and didn’t fight (they laughed later). The battery being dead was a result of the fire, not the reason they ignored it.
---
Q3: How did the neighbors react when they saw the authors?
Correct: They thought the authors were fighting.
Distractors: They offered to help clean up the kitchen. / They laughed at the authors’ cooking skills. / They took the authors to the hospital.
Explanation:
The text says, “They clearly thought we were in a fight,” so this is correct. The other options are unsupported: no mention of help, laughter, or a hospital visit. The neighbors only saw the chaotic scene and assumed the worst.

Thomas was surprised but decided to help. "Secondly," he thought, "I must act fast!" He gathered water and built a wall around the forest. The tiny people cheered, and their leader bowed deeply. "You saved our home!" Thomas smiled, knowing kindness could burn brighter than any fire.

Topic: a fairy tale about a friendly giant who was afraid of mice

Text:
In a small village near a tall tower, there lived a kind giant named Bruno. Though Bruno was gentle and loved helping people, he had one strange fear: mice. The tiny creatures terrified him, and he would hide whenever he heard even the smallest evidence of one. One day, the village president asked Bruno to build a new bridge over the river. Bruno agreed but worried that mice might sneak into his workshop. His teenage twin nephews, Leo and Max, laughed at him. "Don’t be silly, Uncle Bruno!" they said. "Mice are too small to bother you!" But Bruno insisted on keeping his tools in a locked lorry to stay safe. As he worked, a mouse scurried past—click!—Bruno dropped his hammer in fright. The villagers rushed to help, and soon, they all realized Bruno’s fear was silly. The specific sound of the mouse had just been the wind! From that day on, Bruno laughed at his fear and even adopted a tiny mouse named Pip. The bridge was finished on time, and the villagers celebrated with a feast. Bruno learned that bravery isn’t about size—it’s about facing fears, even the smallest ones.

I thought about the most important factor: comfort. The third chair was the best, even if it cost more. My friend laughed and said, "Money doesn’t matter if it’s not good for you!" She said his name was Leo and that he was the president of the local children’s club. I laughed and told him I was happy to meet the “big boss.”

We talked for a bit, and Leo showed me his favorite toy—a small motorcycle. He said he dreamed of riding one like his dad. I told him that one day, he would! His mother smiled and said, “You provide him with so much joy, just by listening.”

That day, I didn’t just buy food—I made a friend. Leo and I exchanged numbers, and now we write to each other. Sometimes, he sends me drawings, and I send him stories. I never expected to meet someone so special in such an unexpected place. It was a small moment, but it changed my trip—and my heart.

Distractors: a loud noise from a motorcycle / a call for help from a tourist / the sound of a spoon falling

Distractors: He was waiting for his mother to return. / He was playing with his toy motorcycle. / He was tired and needed a rest.
Explanation: The text states, “His spoon had fallen into a puddle, and he couldn’t reach it,” so this is the correct reason. Topic: a fairy tale about a rabbit who wanted to learn to fly

Text:

The owl gave Benny a schedule of lessons: "First, you must express your wishes clearly. Then, you’ll learn to jump higher than the tallest trees!" Benny followed the plan. He practiced every day, even when his friends laughed at his silly pants with wings sewn on them. "It doesn’t matter what others think," the owl said. "Believe in your dream!"

One evening, Benny chatted with a wise tortoise who had once been the winner of a flying contest. "I tried to fly too," the tortoise said. "But I copied the birds’ wings wrong. Now I walk slowly—but I’m happy!" Benny listened carefully.

Finally, the day came. Benny stood on a hill, took a deep breath, and—whoosh!—he fell into a bush. The owl sighed. "Flying takes time. But your heart is brave!" Benny smiled. Maybe one day, he’d soar like the wind. For now, he’d keep trying.

Topic: a fairy tale about a lonely cloud that made friends with a mountain

Text:

The mountain, an old stone giant named Rocko, looked up. "Why are you so sad?" Fluffy sighed, "I have no friends." Rocko smiled. "Then let’s progress together! You can teach me about the sky, and I’ll tell you stories of the earth."

At first, Fluffy was nervous. He didn’t know how to talk to a mountain. But Rocko spoke happily in a deep, rumbling voice. "You shall see," he said, "we’ll be great friends!" They spent days laughing as Fluffy threw rain on Rocko’s slopes, making flowers bloom. The villagers below watched in wonder. "What a kind manner!" they whispered.

One evening, Fluffy asked, "Rocko, do you think we’ve made progress?" Rocko nodded. "The best kind—friendship. Now, let’s review our day!" And as the stars appeared, the two friends rested, knowing no one could ever be as happy as them.

Topic: a fairy tale about a mouse who lived in a library and learned to read

Text:
In a quiet library lived a small mouse named Milo whose books were always falling apart. The librarian, Mrs. Hatter, thought Milo was crazy—he spent all day chewing pages, but not to eat! He wanted to learn to read. "This is rubbish!" she sighed, but Milo didn’t care. He had a plan: he would use the library’s dry, dusty books to teach himself.

One day, a virus spread through the library—a sneezing spell that turned all the books’ letters into tiny, flying dots. Milo’s nose itched too, but he saw his chance! He chased the dots, catching them in his paws and arranging them into words. "The exact opposite of chaos!" he cheered. The librarian watched in shock as Milo read his first sentence: "A mouse who reads is never lost."

By winter, Milo had memorized the system of letters. Mrs. Hatter, impressed, let him stay—even though he still chewed the corners of books. "You’re the worst library pest," she laughed, "but you’ll pay for this someday!" Milo just grinned. He knew he’d already won.

Correct: He spent all day chewing books without eating them.
Distractors: He stole books from the library. / He refused to follow the library rules. / He tried to fly like the book dots.

The text says Milo "spent all day chewing pages, but not to eat!"—this shows his unusual (and seemingly crazy) behavior. Stealing books or breaking rules isn’t mentioned; Milo’s goal was to learn, not cause trouble. The "flying dots" were part of the virus, not something Milo did.

Correct: A sneezing virus turned letters into flying dots he could catch.
Distractors: The librarian taught him secretly. / He memorized the books by heart. / He used a special reading system from another mouse.
Explanation:
The text explains the virus made letters "tiny, flying dots" that Milo chased and rearranged. There’s no mention of the librarian teaching him, or Milo memorizing whole books—he focused on letters. No other mouse or system is referenced.
---
Q3: How did Mrs. Hatter feel about Milo in the end?
Correct: She was impressed by his progress, despite calling him a pest.
Distractors: She kicked him out of the library. / She decided to help him chew books faster. / She pretended nothing had happened.
Explanation:
The text says she "let him stay" after seeing his reading success, even joking that he was the "worst library pest." This shows she was amused but approving. She didn’t kick him out, help him chew books, or ignore his actions—she acknowledged his effort.

Soon, Liam’s friends teased him, calling him unhappy because he spent all day talking to the wind instead of playing. But Liam didn’t care. Now, I’m proud of how far I’ve come. My family says I’ve improved strongly, and I even performed at a small concert last month. Learning guitar wasn’t easy, but it was worth every effort.

Topic: an adventure story about a mountain trip

Text:
Last summer, Jake and his friends planned a three-day hike up Mount Eldridge. The weather forecast was prediction—clear skies and warm temperatures—but Jake’s older brother, Mark, warned them it could be wrong. "Don’t trust those reports," Mark said, shaking his head. "Last year, we got caught in a storm, and my camera’s frame broke when I dropped it."

Jake didn’t want to listen. "We’ll be fine," he insisted, ignoring Mark’s protest. His friends laughed, and they set off, playing a loud drum beat on a small hand drum to keep their spirits up. But by the second day, the wind picked up, and Jake felt embarrassed when his tent collapsed. "This is your fault for ignoring Mark!" his friend Leo judged, pointing at him.

That night, as they huddled together, Jake realized Mark had been right. "I’m sorry," Jake admitted. "He wasn’t our enemy—he was just trying to help." The group agreed to turn back early the next morning. On the way down, Jake noticed a practical solution: they could use Mark’s old map instead of relying on their phones. By the time they reached the bottom, Jake was glad they’d listened—even if it meant admitting he’d been wrong.

Q1: Why did Jake feel embarrassed during the hike?
Correct: His tent collapsed because of the bad weather.
Distractors: He lost his camera’s frame. / He argued with his friends. / He forgot to bring a drum.
Explanation:
The text states, "the wind picked up, and Jake felt embarrassed when his tent collapsed." This directly explains why he felt embarrassed. The other options are incorrect because:
- Losing the camera’s frame happened earlier (Mark’s mistake), not Jake’s.
- Arguing with friends isn’t mentioned as a cause of embarrassment.
- Forgetting the drum isn’t relevant to his embarrassment.
---
Q2: What did Leo accuse Jake of?
Correct: Ignoring Mark’s warning.
Distractors: Being their enemy. / Breaking the drum. / Judging Mark unfairly.
Explanation:
Leo said, "This is your fault for ignoring Mark!" This shows Leo blamed Jake for not listening to Mark. The other options are wrong because:
- Calling Jake their "enemy" is false—Mark was the one trying to help.
- Breaking the drum isn’t mentioned.
- Judging Mark unfairly was Mark’s role, not Jake’s.
---
Q3: How did Jake and his friends solve their problem on the last day?
Correct: They used Mark’s old map instead of their phones.
Distractors: They protested against Mark’s advice. / They continued the hike despite the storm. / They blamed the weather for their mistakes.
Explanation:
The text explains, "Jake noticed a practical solution: they could use Mark’s old map instead of relying on their phones." The other options are incorrect because:
- Protesting against Mark’s advice wasn’t their solution.
- Continuing the hike despite the storm would have been dangerous (they turned back).
- Blaming the weather isn’t mentioned as their action.
---
Q4: What lesson did Jake learn from the trip?
Correct: Mark was right to warn them, and they should have listened.
Distractors: Trusting weather forecasts is always wrong. / Hiking with friends is never practical. / Admitting mistakes makes you weak.
Explanation:
Jake admits, "I’m sorry... He wasn’t our enemy—he was just trying to help." This shows he learned to value Mark’s advice. The other options are wrong because:
- The text doesn’t say all weather forecasts are wrong—just that they should have double-checked.
- Hiking with friends is described as fun, not impractical.
- Admitting mistakes isn’t called "weak"; Jake’s apology shows maturity.

A year later, Liam returned to the track—not as a marathoner, but as a sprinter. His latest achievement was winning a regional championship in the 100-meter dash. Though his need for speed had changed, his determination had not. "I didn’t just come back," Liam said. "I came back stronger."

Topic: a story about a volunteer experience

Text:
Last summer, I joined a team of volunteers helping to clean up after a terrible explosion in a small town. The substance that had caused the damage was highly toxic, so we had to wear protective suits and masks to avoid inhaling any dangerous particles. The air was thick with smoke, and it was hard to breathe without feeling dizzy. My first task was to check the safety of the area, making sure no one was in contact with contaminated water or soil.

One day, while working near a damaged building, I heard a loud noise. A small piece of metal had fallen, and I barely had time to duck before it nearly hit me. My heart was racing, and I took a deep breath to calm down. The team leader, who had years of experience in emergencies, explained that this was part of the battle against the disaster. "We can’t let fear stop us," he said. "Our intelligence and teamwork are what will save lives here."

By the end of the week, we had made a real profit for the community—not in money, but in safety. The town’s mayor thanked us, saying our work had given people back their breath—both literally and figuratively. I learned that volunteering isn’t just about helping others; it’s also about discovering your own strength. The definition of courage, I realized, isn’t just about facing danger—it’s about staying focused when everything around you seems to be falling apart.

Questions:
Q1: What was the main challenge the volunteers faced during the cleanup?
Correct: Difficulty breathing due to toxic air.
Distractors: Lack of proper tools to remove the substance. / Fear of wild animals in the damaged area. / Insufficient food and water supplies.
Explanation: The text states, "The air was thick with smoke, and it was hard to breathe without feeling dizzy." This directly describes the difficulty breathing as the main challenge. The other options are not mentioned—there’s no mention of missing tools, wild animals, or food shortages.
---
Q2: Why did the team leader say, "We can’t let fear stop us"?
Correct: To encourage the volunteers to keep working despite the dangers.
Distractors: To explain why the explosion happened in the first place. / To criticize those who were not working hard enough. / To warn about the risk of another explosion.
Explanation: The leader’s words come after describing the near-miss with falling metal, showing he was motivating the team. The text says, "The team leader... explained that this was part of the battle against the disaster." The other options are incorrect because the text doesn’t discuss causes of the explosion, criticize individuals, or warn about future explosions.
---
Q3: What did the mayor’s thanks imply about the volunteers’ work?
Correct: Their efforts restored safety and peace to the town.
Distractors: They had earned a large financial reward for their services. / They had discovered a way to neutralize the toxic substance. / They had rebuilt the damaged buildings themselves.
Explanation: The mayor said, "our work had given people back their breath—both literally and figuratively." This suggests the volunteers’ work improved safety and well-being. The other options are incorrect because the text mentions no financial reward, scientific discovery, or physical rebuilding by the volunteers.
---
Q4: According to the narrator, what is the true meaning of courage in this situation?
Correct: Staying focused when facing overwhelming challenges.
Distractors: Being completely fearless in dangerous situations. / Only helping others without expecting anything in return. / Knowing exactly how to handle every emergency.
Explanation: The narrator concludes, "The definition of courage... isn’t just about facing danger—it’s about staying focused when everything around you seems to be falling apart." This matches the correct answer. The other options are incorrect because courage isn’t defined as fearlessness (the narrator admits to fear), selflessness without expectation (the text doesn’t mention this), or perfect knowledge (the leader had experience, but the narrator didn’t claim to know everything).

She says, "Creativity is not just for the talented—it’s for anyone willing to try." Her workshops focus on fitness of the mind, encouraging participants to think differently.

One of her most famous pieces, "The Pull of the Sea," was inspired by a stormy day at the beach. Lena spent hours watching waves crash against the shore, feeling the raw power of nature. "Art is about capturing emotions," she says, "and sometimes, you just need to step outside to find them."

The crowd cheered, but the real surprise came at the end. The "ghost" was actually Jake, who had secretly recovered and played the whole time, hiding his injury. The team had been punished for their doubt, and the lesson was clear: trust matters more than perfection.

The movie’s twist made me think about how poisonous distrust can be. After the credits rolled, many viewers were frightened by the ending, but also inspired. The film reminded me that sometimes, the best stories come from believing in something—even when it seems imaginary.

I tried to analyse the patterns, but my friend just stood there, mouth open. "It’s like nothing I’ve ever seen!" he said.

There was a strange similarity between the lights and a firework display, but more alive, more natural. The owner had told us that the best time to see them is between October and March, when the nights are longest. We eventually returned to the cottage, exhausted but happy. The Northern Lights had attracted us with their magic, and we knew we’d be back.

Topic: a news article about linguists documenting endangered languages

Linguists from around the world are racing against time to save languages that are disappearing. In a small village in Papua New Guinea, a team recorded the last speakers of a language called Yélî Dnye, which has only about 200 unemployed farmers left who still speak it daily. The researchers were amazed by how quickly the language was fading—some elders used to sing traditional songs in Yélî Dnye, but now even children struggle to understand them.

One linguist, Dr. Elena Vasquez, explained that many young people in the village now prefer the national language, leading to a heavily reduced use of Yélî Dnye. "We’re documenting every word, every phrase, before it’s too late," she said. The team even traveled to a nearby stadium to film elders demonstrating how to bend bamboo in old traditions, using costumes from centuries ago.

The project isn’t just about words—it’s about culture. Vasquez hopes that by preserving Yélî Dnye, they can also shift the community’s focus back to their heritage. "Some people think this work is hopeless," she admitted, "but we’re brave enough to try." Meanwhile, a brand-new app is being developed to teach the language to younger generations, combining old recordings with modern technology.

"You’re my brother," Liam said. The man sighed and admitted the truth: he was Liam’s twin, separated at birth. They had spent their lives believing they were orphans, but fate had brought them together.

Liam was glad but also confused. "Why didn’t you look for me?" he asked. The man explained that he had always hoped someone would find him. Now, they could finally live as brothers. As they sat by the lake that evening, Liam realized this was the greatest adventure of his life—not just discovering a twin, but rediscovering himself.

With love,

Topic: a story about a person who inspired me

Text:
When I was a child, my grandmother used to tell me stories about her friend, Clara Barton. Clara was an official nurse during the American Civil War, and she was known for her talent in helping soldiers who had been poisoned by bad food or injuries. She worked tirelessly, even when others were embarrassed to help. One day, she saw a group of wounded soldiers left without care. Instead of turning away, she pushed through her fear and started treating them. Her bravery determined her future—she later became the founder of the American Red Cross.

Clara’s story inspired me to never give up, even when things seem difficult. At school, I struggled with math, but my teacher, an editor at a local newspaper, noticed my talent for writing. She suggested I join the school’s newspaper program. At first, I was embarrassed because I thought my writing wasn’t good enough. But she told me, “Every great writer starts somewhere.” I took her advice and began writing short stories after school. Over time, my confidence grew, and I even won a small contest.

One day, my teacher asked me to help with the school newspaper’s shift from print to digital. I was nervous, but I remembered Clara’s courage. I worked hard, learning new skills, and soon, I was helping edit articles. Now, I want to be like Clara—someone who pushes through challenges and makes a difference. Her story taught me that talent and determination can change lives, even if you start with just a little stuff (like a notebook and a dream).

Topic: a story about a very noisy neighbor and how I solved the problem

Text:

Every morning at 6 AM, his loud music would attract complaints from other tenants. I tried talking to him, but he just shrugged and said, “It’s my headline moment!” I couldn’t breathe peacefully, and my walls felt like they were sinking under the noise.

One day, I had an idea. I pulled out my paintbrushes and decorated my door with bright, cheerful murals—flowers, birds, and even a “Good Morning!” sign. I left it open so he’d see it. The next morning, he knocked on my door, frozen in surprise. “Wow, your place looks amazing!” he said. I smiled and said, “Thanks! I was inspired by the spirit of this building. Maybe we could reflect that in our routines?” He laughed, turned down his music, and even helped me add a few touches to my door.

Now, we breathe easier together. The park view is still beautiful, and Mr. Thompson’s music is finally at a reasonable volume. Sometimes, he even plays soft jazz to match my decorate-friendly vibe.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator initially dislike Mr. Thompson?
Correct: His loud music woke everyone up early.
Distractors: He never greeted the narrator. / He played music at night. / He refused to fix his broken door.
Explanation: The text states, “Every morning at 6 AM, his loud music would attract complaints,” proving the issue was the noise waking people up. The other options are unsupported—there’s no mention of nighttime music, broken doors, or lack of greetings.
---

Distractors: They wrote a formal complaint to the building manager. / They installed soundproofing panels. / They moved to a quieter apartment.
Explanation: The text describes “I pulled out my paintbrushes and decorated my door,” confirming the creative solution. The other options are never mentioned or implied.
---
Q3: How did Mr. Thompson react when he saw the narrator’s door?
Correct: He was surprised and complimented the decorations.
Distractors: He ignored it and kept playing music louder. / He asked the narrator to remove the art. / He offered to pay for the paint.
Explanation: The text says, “He laughed, turned down his music, and even helped me add a few touches,” showing his positive reaction. The other options contradict the peaceful resolution described.

Explanation: The text concludes, “Now, we breathe easier together. [...] His music is finally at a reasonable volume,” proving the volume adjustment. The other options are false—no move is mentioned, their relationship improved, and silence isn’t the goal.

The event organizer, Ms. Carter, frowned. "This is unfair to the speaker," she muttered.

Liam’s mind raced. He could apologize, but the damage was done. The audience laughed at the jokes about plants instead of his carefully researched topic on renewable energy. By the time he finished, his voice cracked. Later, Jake clapped him on the back. "At least you didn’t cry," he said. Liam groaned. "Next time, I’ll triple-check my emails."

The villagers treated him like a local legend, though some called him a victim of his own imagination. When I asked why they didn’t vote him out of the village council (where he held a minor position), he just chuckled. "They need someone mad enough to keep things interesting!"

I left feeling both inspired and slightly confused. Was Mr. Nguyen a genius or just a dreamer? Maybe a little of both.

Worse, his old friends teased him for not being honest. Liam realized his small lie had grown into a big problem. He had to race against time to fix his mistake before his reputation was ruined.

Topic: a story about a time I realized I was wrong about something

Text:

The restaurant became so popular that people waited for hours just to get a table. My friend, who had worked hard to make it a success, even won an award for his effort.

One day, I decided to visit the restaurant to see for myself. I was surprised by how busy it was—every table was full, and the atmosphere was lively. My friend told me that his aim was to bring eastern cuisine to our small town, and he had succeeded beyond his wildest dreams. I realized that my prediction was based on outdated ideas. I also discovered that he had spent months training his staff to provide excellent service. My friend laughed when I admitted I had been wrong. He said that sometimes, people cheat themselves by refusing to see new opportunities.

Now, I visit the restaurant every week. I even tried sushi for the first time and loved it! The experience taught me that I should never judge something without giving it a real chance. My friend’s success proved that hard work and creativity can turn even the most unlikely plot into a story of triumph.

Topic: a story about an unusual skill that helped me in life

Text:
When I was a child, my father worked as a firefighter, and I often visited the fire station. One day, I saw a small sculpture made of metal near the entrance—a twisted, melted shape that looked like a needle after an explosion. The firefighters told me it was a piece of a truck that had exploded during a rescue mission. I was fascinated by how something so dangerous could create something so strange and beautiful.

Years later, I realized this experience taught me an important lesson: consequence can lead to unexpected results. When I was 18, I got a job on a cargo ship as a deckhand. One night, a storm hit, and the transport ship’s ropes snapped. We had to escape quickly, but the waves were too strong. I remembered the sculpture from my childhood—the way metal could bend but still hold its shape—and I tied a loose rope around a broken pipe to create a makeshift ladder. It wasn’t perfect, but it worked. The crew climbed to safety, and I realized that thinking slow and carefully in a crisis could save lives.

Now, I use this skill in my work as a safety inspector. When accidents happen, I don’t panic. Instead, I look for the "sculpture" in the chaos—the unexpected solution hidden in the consequence. My unusual skill isn’t just about ropes or explosions; it’s about seeing beauty and opportunity in unexpected places.

Topic: a story about an unusual reading club

Text:
In the quiet town of Meadowgrove, there was an unusual reading club called Backwards Books. Unlike most clubs, they didn’t read books from start to finish—they read them backwards, beginning with the last chapter. The idea came from their coach, Mr. Holloway, who believed that reading this way made people think honestly about the story’s ending first.

The club met every Thursday in the central library, where members appreciated the challenge. Some suffered at first, struggling to follow plots without knowing how they began. But Mr. Holloway insisted on a proper approach: they had to read carefully, even if it felt strange. "You used to read forward," he’d say with a smile. "Now, you’re learning to see things differently."

One member, Clara, loved the club. "It’s like solving a puzzle," she said. "You guess why the story ends the way it does, then check if you were right." Others disagreed, calling it confusing. But Mr. Holloway argued that their way made readers more creative. "Most people never question their first impressions," he said. "Here, you question everything—including ours."

Text:

One evening, Liam vanished during a storm. When he reappeared, his clothes were soaked, and his hands were shaking. Clara hugged him tightly. "You surely shouldn’t risk this," she said. Liam nodded, realizing that while his ability was amazing, it wasn’t worth the fear it caused. From then on, he used it only in emergencies, keeping his secret safe.

When her turn came, she chanted softly, and the ground beneath her feet split open. A small stone vanished into the depths. The judges counted the seconds—45. It was enough.

Max’s spell failed when his incantation backfired, sending a puff of smoke into the crowd. Lena won by a point, earning the diamond wand and a standing ovation. As she held the prize, she realized magic wasn’t just about power—it was about believing in something beautiful, even if the world called it impossible.

Mr. Carter wasn’t just proud—he was calm knowing he’d reached someone who had made him feel disappointed at first.

Topic: a story about unusual trends in interior design

Text:
In recent years, interior design has seen some highly unusual trends that challenge traditional styles. One of the most surprising is the use of leather in unexpected ways. While leather is often associated with furniture, designers are now covering walls, ceilings, and even floors with it. The rough texture adds a bold, industrial feel to spaces, making them look both modern and edgy. Critics compare this trend to the 1970s, when leather was popular in nightclubs, but today’s designs are far more refined.

Another trend involves bringing elements of nature indoors. Instead of using artificial plants, designers are incorporating real soil into decor. One famous example is a living wall made of earth, where small plants grow directly from the surface. This not only adds greenery but also creates a unique, organic atmosphere. A headline in a recent design magazine called this "the future of eco-friendly interiors," and many homeowners are eager to try it.

For those who love technology, some designers are experimenting with helicopter-themed rooms. Yes, you read that right! These spaces feature miniature helicopter models, sound systems that mimic engine noises, and even ceiling fans shaped like rotors. While it may sound extreme, the idea is to create a futuristic, adrenaline-filled environment. One designer explained, "We want people to feel like they’re stepping into a sci-fi movie." The trend has even inspired a quotation from a famous architect: "Design should surprise, and this does just that."

Finally, some designers are using AI to assist in creating custom furniture. Instead of mass-produced pieces, clients can now input their preferences, and an algorithm generates unique designs. This trend represents a shift toward personalization, where every home can reflect its owner’s individuality.

The next day, she handed the map to the police, who arrested Lord Harroway for fraud. The newspapers called Clara "the most clever servant in Oldtown." Though she lost her job, she used her share of the treasure to educate herself and open a small school for poor children in the neighbourhood.

The team was thrilled, but Elena felt embarrassing when she admitted she had almost missed it because she was distracted by a rugby match she had watched the night before.

The exhibition also included a section on how merchants used clever tricks to escape bandits, like hiding goods in hollow statues. It was fascinating to see how trade shaped cultures.

However, some people might ignore the smaller exhibits, like the delicate pottery, because the golden masks and jewels hang in the center, drawing all the attention. Still, the exhibition left a lasting impression—one that made me want to learn more about these forgotten stories.

A little stream runs beside it, and in summer, I can sit by the water with a cold drink and watch the sunset.

I’m not getting any younger, so I want this house before I’m too old to enjoy it. My wife still laughs when I talk about it, but I know she’ll love it too—once she sees it.

Photographers on Earth often capture stunning images of the night sky, but none compare to the views astronauts get from space. Even clients of space agencies dream of one day traveling beyond our planet, though legal restrictions currently limit such adventures to a lucky few.

Topic: an article about the unusual New Year traditions in different cultures

Text:
New Year’s Eve is a time for celebration, but some traditions around the world are far from familiar. In Denmark, people smash old plates against their neighbors’ doors—a slow but exciting way to welcome the new year. The more broken pieces, the luckier the year will be! Meanwhile, in Greece, families gather to bury a small doll made of cloth under their home’s threshold. This symbolizes leaving behind bad luck and starting fresh.

In Spain, eating 12 grapes at midnight is a must—one for each knock of the clock. If you finish them all, you’ll have good fortune. But in Brazil, people wear yellow underwear for luck, while in the Philippines, they range from jumping off chairs to eating sticky rice for prosperity. Some traditions, like Scotland’s Hogmanay, involve singing and dancing until sunrise, ensuring no one feels lonely.

Not all customs are affordable, though. In Russia, throwing water on friends and family is common, but in Japan, people visit temples to pray for health. The basis of these traditions? A mix of history, superstition, and hope for a better year ahead.

Q1: What do Danes believe about broken plates during New Year’s Eve?
Correct: The more plates are smashed, the luckier the year will be.
Distractors: They use them to decorate their homes. / They believe it brings bad luck. / They only break them if they are expensive.
Explanation:
The text states: "In Denmark, people smash old plates against their neighbors’ doors—a slow but exciting way to welcome the new year. The more broken pieces, the luckier the year will be!" This confirms that breaking plates is linked to luck. The other options contradict the text: plates are not used for decoration, breaking them is not considered bad luck, and their cost is irrelevant.
---

Even within our own countries, hidden gems exist. In Australia, Pinnacles Desert has sharp rock formations that resemble a further galaxy. Locals joke that an agent from NASA might secretly visit to study them. These places remind us that Earth itself can feel like an alien world—without ever leaving it.

For instance, swapping white rice for brown rice isn’t a sacrifice—it’s an upgrade. Some version of healthy eating involves cutting out entire food groups, but experts agree that moderation works best.

One famous experiment involved a group of people who tried to eat only organic cotton-based foods (yes, that’s a joke!). The point is, healthy eating doesn’t have to be extreme. Small changes, like drinking more water or choosing grilled over fried foods, can lead to long-term success. The goal isn’t perfection—it’s progress.

Surprisingly, some dangers are hidden—like the risk of explosions in oil rigs or the dangers of working near open pots of boiling chemicals. The following safety measures are critical: training, protective gear, and strict protocols.

Experts summarize the rules simply: no hands can touch the pin, and the suit worn must be waterproof. The first person to reach the top without dropping the pin wins a trophy.

One recommendation from past participants is to practice breathing exercises before attempting the climb. The wind and rain make balance nearly impossible, but the thrill keeps people coming back. Whether you’re a sailor or just an adventurer, this challenge proves that some sports are meant for the truly fearless.

By kilometer 15, I was running smoothly, and the finish line appeared like a beacon.

Later, I learned that the camera was from the 1950s and could be worth a lot of money. I had bought something valuable without even realizing it.

Now, I’m thinking of selling it to buy a better camera for my own photography. But part of me wants to keep it—it’s a piece of history, and it reminds me of my grandfather’s stories.

My energy improved, and I started admiring people who ran long distances—especially when I saw a stranger jogging at 6 a.m. in the park.

Now, jogging is totally part of my routine. I no longer see it as a chore; it’s something I look forward to. The key was making it simple and finding small ways to stay inspired.

Different generations handle stress in various ways, but the key is finding what works for you. Some people knock out stress with exercise, while others prefer meditation or talking to a friend. Remember, stress doesn’t have to control you; you can take charge with small, smart choices.

Topic: an article about the advantages and disadvantages of online shopping

Text:
Online shopping has become equally popular as traditional shopping, but it has both advantages and disadvantages. One of the biggest benefits is convenience—you can buy almost anything from home without leaving your couch. Many people are amazed by how quickly products arrive at their doorstep, especially when shopping successfully during sales. However, some shoppers get upset when items don’t match their expectations or when deliveries are delayed.

Another issue is the lack of personal staff assistance. In physical stores, you can ask for help, but online, you often rely on chatbots or customer service emails, which can be annoying if they don’t respond quickly. Some people also struggle with returns, as they must follow complicated processes to send items back.

On the other hand, online shopping saves time—no need to wait in long till lines or search for products on crowded shelves. Many retailers intend to make shopping easier, but technical problems or stock shortages can ruin the experience. Overall, while online shopping offers flexibility, it’s important to weigh its pros and cons before deciding which method suits you best.

The day of the election for our local council, I met Mark at the market. He pushed me to sell it quickly, saying, "Don’t repeat your mistakes—this guitar isn’t doing you any good." I laughed but knew he was right. The deal was simple: cash in hand, and the guitar was his.

As we walked away, I realized how sensible it was to let go. The money went toward a new instrument, and I felt lighter, like I’d taken the first step on a new path. Now, I play my new guitar every day, and sometimes, I even think about how my old one might be inspiring someone else’s music.

Topic: a personal story about an unusual, but effective way to plan a budget

Text:
When I was struggling to save money, my grandfather shared an unusual trick that worked forever. Instead of using apps or spreadsheets, he suggested a hands-on approach. Every morning, I placed a coin in a jar labeled "Savings." But here’s the twist: I had to touch the jar every time I spent money. If I took out cash, I moved a coin from the jar to my pocket. Generally, people avoid investigating their spending habits, but this method forced me to think twice before buying anything unnecessary.

One day, I noticed my savings jar was getting lighter, so I decided to open a bank account and transfer the coins regularly. The leading idea was to see my savings grow visually—like watching a tail of coins turn into bills. Over time, I realized this method made budgeting feel less like a chore and more like an outdoors adventure, where every coin was a small victory. Now, I generally keep a few coins in a jar as a reminder of how simple saving can be.

The other options (app, notebook, automatic transfers) are not mentioned and contradict the hands-on approach described.

Topic: an article about polar days and nights

Text:
In the Arctic and Antarctic, the sun never sets during the summer—a phenomenon called the polar day. For months, viewers of the midnight sun can enjoy 24 hours of daylight, making it practical for outdoor activities like hiking or fishing. Conversely, the polar night occurs in winter, when the sun never rises, leaving only dim light from the sky. Scientists use a diagram to show how the Earth’s tilt affects these extremes.

While polar days and nights are fascinating, they also impact daily life. For example, farmers must adjust planting times, and animals like polar bears rely on the sun’s bite of light to hunt. Some communities even hold elections during polar days to take advantage of the extra daylight. However, not all regions experience equal conditions—secondary factors like latitude and weather play a role.

Tourists often judge the best time to visit based on these cycles, hoping to profit from the unique experience. Whether it’s chasing the midnight sun or surviving the long night, polar regions offer a practical lesson in Earth’s natural rhythms.

Distractors: Polar days are warmer than polar nights. / Polar nights last longer than polar days. / Polar days only occur in the Arctic, while polar nights happen in both poles.
Explanation:
The text states that polar days have "24 hours of daylight" and polar nights have "no sunrise," making the first option correct. The other options are incorrect because:
- Warmer temperatures are not mentioned.
- Duration is not compared—both depend on location and season.
- Geographical exclusivity is false; both phenomena occur in both poles.

Topic: an article about the surprising facts about bird migration

Text:
Bird migration is one of nature’s most fascinating phenomena, yet many facts about it remain surprising. For example, some birds, like the Arctic tern, travel over 60,000 miles each year—an odd feat considering their small size. Scientists have also discovered a similarity between bird migration and human travel: just as people file through airports, birds follow precise routes, often flying in a V-formation to reduce wind resistance.

One pale but well-documented fact is that some birds, like the European cuckoo, rely on the Earth’s magnetic field to navigate. However, this ability isn’t perfect—bad weather or artificial lights can annoy their internal compass, leading them astray. Migration also has its disadvantages: many birds face exhaustion, predators, or even collisions with human structures like wind turbines.

Interestingly, bird migration has inspired campaigns to protect them. While some species, like swallows, are fashionable symbols of spring, others, such as the whooping crane, are endangered. Ancient literature, including Greek myths, often described birds as messengers, highlighting their historical importance. Today, scientists continue to study migration to uncover more secrets about these incredible travelers.

Topic: an article about unusual alternative sources of energy
